D) btrfs — required for Docker on SUSE Linux
✅ Correct Answer: C

overlay2 is the default and recommended driver for all modern Linux distributions. It's included in the mainline kernel (4.0+), stable, performant, and supports all Docker features. Legacy drivers: aufs (deprecated), devicemapper (deprecated), btrfs (filesystem-specific). Don't change the storage driver unless you have a specific reason.

📝 Detailed Explanation
Storage Driver Selection — What to Use
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  DRIVER        │ STATUS        │ USE CASE                        │
  │  ──────────────┼───────────────┼─────────────────────────────── │
  │  overlay2      │ ✅ DEFAULT    │ All modern Linux (4.0+)         │
  │  fuse-overlayfs│ ✅ Rootless   │ Rootless Docker (user namesp) │
  │  btrfs         │ ⚠️ Specific  │ Only if /var/lib/docker is     │
  │                │               │ on a Btrfs filesystem          │
  │  devicemapper  │ ❌ DEPRECATED │ Do not use for new installs    │
  │  aufs          │ ❌ DEPRECATED │ Do not use for new installs    │
  │  overlay       │ ❌ DEPRECATED │ Use overlay2 instead           │
  │  ──────────────┴───────────────┴─────────────────────────────── │
  │                                                                 │
  │  $ docker info | grep "Storage Driver"                          │
  │  Storage Driver: overlay2                                       │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

💡 DCA Exam Tip: overlay2 = default and recommended. aufs/devicemapper = deprecated. fuse-overlayfs = for rootless Docker. Check with docker info. Don't change the storage driver unless necessary — overlay2 is the right choice for virtually all deployments.

Storage Q199 of 200
Why is the devicemapper storage driver deprecated?
A) It was too fast and caused SSD wear issues
B) It requires dedicated block devices, has complex configuration (thin pools), performance issues in loopback mode, and overlay2 is simpler and faster with kernel-native support
C) It only works on Ubuntu and not on other distributions
D) It doesn't support container networking
✅ Correct Answer: B

devicemapper was Docker's early enterprise storage driver. It required complex configuration: dedicated block devices, thin provisioning pools, and LVM setup. In loopback mode (default for testing), it was slow and prone to issues. overlay2, using kernel-native OverlayFS, is simpler, faster, and requires no special configuration — hence devicemapper was deprecated.

📝 Detailed Explanation
devicemapper vs overlay2 — Why overlay2 Won
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  DEVICEMAPPER (deprecated):            OVERLAY2 (current):       │
  │  ┌──────────────────────────────┐     ┌──────────────────────┐ │
  │  │ • Requires LVM thin pool     │     │ • Kernel-native (no  │ │
  │  │ • Dedicated block device     │     │   extra setup)       │ │
  │  │ • Complex config (daemon.json│     │ • Works on any FS    │ │
  │  │ • Loopback mode: SLOW, only  │     │ • Simple, fast       │ │
  │  │   for testing                │     │ • Default since 18.09│ │
  │  │ • Production: direct-lvm     │     │ • Actively maintained│ │
  │  │   (complex setup)            │     └──────────────────────┘ │
  │  └──────────────────────────────┘                               │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

💡 DCA Exam Tip: devicemapper = deprecated. Don't use it for new installs. overlay2 = the replacement. Know the history: devicemapper was the enterprise driver before overlay2 became stable. The DCA may ask about deprecated drivers to test if you know the current best practice.

Storage Q200 of 200
What is the best practice for persistent storage when running Docker on AWS EC2?
A) Store all data in the container's writable layer — it's backed by EBS
B) Use instance store (ephemeral) — it's the fastest option
C) Use S3 as a filesystem mounted into containers
D) Use EBS volumes mounted via Docker volume drivers (rexray) or as bind mounts — EBS provides persistent, snapshot-capable block storage that survives instance termination
✅ Correct Answer: D

For persistent data on AWS EC2, use EBS volumes. Options: (1) Docker volume plugin (rexray/ebs) for dynamic provisioning, (2) Pre-attached EBS volumes as bind mounts, (3) For Swarm, use EBS-backed volume drivers for multi-node access. Instance store is ephemeral (lost on stop). S3 is object storage, not a filesystem.

📝 Detailed Explanation
Docker on AWS — Persistent Storage Architecture
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  AWS CLOUD                                                        │
  │  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
  │  │  EC2 INSTANCE                                             │  │
  │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │  │
  │  │  │ Docker Container (anihpj-db)                        │  │  │
  │  │  │ -v pgdata:/var/lib/postgresql/data                  │  │  │
  │  │  │                                                     │  │  │
  │  │  │ Volume Driver: rexray/ebs                            │  │  │
  │  │  │        │                                            │  │  │
  │  │  │        ▼                                            │  │  │
  │  │  │  EBS VOLUME (gp3, 100GB)                            │  │  │
  │  │  │  • Persistent (survives container/instance restart) │  │  │
  │  │  │  • Snapshot-capable (backups)                       │  │  │
  │  │  │  • Can be detached and re-attached to new instance  │  │  │
  │  │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │  │
  │  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
  │                                                                 │
  │  ALTERNATIVE: Use RDS (managed PostgreSQL) — no volume management│
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

💡 DCA Exam Tip: AWS persistent storage = EBS (block) or EFS (NFS). EBS via rexray driver or bind mount. EFS for multi-node shared storage. Instance store = ephemeral (NOT persistent). For databases in production, consider RDS instead of managing your own volumes.
